01. raw-007 / Backward Alarm Clock
candidateId: sem1-bbdb302fefb48bd5

PROTAGONIST:
Opa

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Wake up at 7 AM after setting alarm

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Alarm clock

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Alarm rings at set time; hands move forward.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The clock's hands move backward whenever the alarm is set, so time appears to run in reverse; the alarm rings only when the hands return to midnight.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Opa sets alarm for 7:00, the hour hand starts cranking counterclockwise.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Clock is meant to measure forward time; reversing hands makes scheduled wake-up impossible.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
Setting the alarm to a future time causes the clock to move backward away from that time, so the alarm will never ring at 7 AM.

FRAME ZERO:
Opa sits up in bed, staring at a bedside clock whose hour hand is moving backward past 6, while the alarm hand points to 7.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Opa manually winds the clock forward to 6:59, expecting the alarm soon.
Visible consequence: As soon as he releases, the hands spin backward rapidly to 3:00.
New information: Manual forward adjustment is undone by the reverse mechanism.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Opa turns the alarm off and just watches the clock to see what time it shows.
Visible consequence: The hands freeze, then twitch backward one minute for every real second that passes.
New information: Without alarm set, the clock still runs backward but slower.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Opa sets the alarm for a time that already appears behind the current hand position, like 2:00, hoping it will trigger quickly.
Visible consequence: The alarm rings immediately, but the hands jump forward to noon, and the ringing reverses itself into a silent vacuum of sound.
New information: Triggering the alarm causes time to jump forward to noon and sound to invert.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Opa unplugs the clock and uses his phone to check the actual time.
Visible consequence: The clock's display reappears on his phone screen, showing the same backward time, and the phone's alarm icon turns into an hourglass.
New information: The clock's impossible rule spreads to other time-keeping devices.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Opa gives up and falls back asleep.
Visible consequence: The clock chimes twelve times in reverse, and Opa wakes up at 7 AM, but the clock reads 7 PM.
New information: The backward time eventually catches up to the correct wake hour, but day and night are swapped.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The clock chimes twelve times in reverse, and Opa wakes up at 7 AM, but the clock reads 7 PM.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
If Opa trusts the clock, he'll sleep through day; if he ignores it, he wakes on time but distrusts all clocks.

ENVIRONMENT: Bedroom with bedside clock
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

02. raw-013 / Tangling Hairbrush
candidateId: sem1-8d1da895a1fb41af

PROTAGONIST:
Opa

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Brush hair smooth

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Hairbrush

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Brushing untangles hair and smooths it.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Each brush stroke creates a new knot, and the knots tighten the more you brush; hair eventually coils around the brush like a topiary.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Opa brushes once, hair stands up in a giant knot.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Brush bristles are meant to separate strands; if they weave strands together, brushing becomes tangling.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action of brushing makes hair worse, so smooth hair is impossible.

FRAME ZERO:
Opa stands in front of mirror holding a brush, but his hair is a twisted bird's nest with the brush embedded in it.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Opa pulls the brush out slowly, hoping to unwind the knot.
Visible consequence: The knot tightens into a bun that pulls his head back.
New information: Pulling the brush out causes the hair to cinch, not loosen.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Opa uses a wide-tooth comb to gently work through the tangle.
Visible consequence: The comb adds more knots, and his hair expands into a giant sphere around his head.
New information: Any grooming tool multiplies knots instead of removing them.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Opa applies hair conditioner to make strands slippery.
Visible consequence: The hair becomes like rubber, and the brush bounces off, but the knots stretch and snap back with more loops.
New information: Conditioner turns hair elastic, making tangles spring-loaded.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Opa puts on a hat to hide the hair and stop brushing.
Visible consequence: The hat is sucked into the hair-knot and swallowed, disappearing into the tangled mass.
New information: The hair's tangling now acts like a living trap, consuming objects.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Opa uses scissors to cut the hair away.
Visible consequence: The hair regrows instantly into a larger, tighter knot, trapping the scissors.
New information: Cutting triggers accelerated regrowth with more tangles.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The hair regrows instantly into a larger, tighter knot, trapping the scissors.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Opa can try different tools, but the hair always re-tangles and often captures the tool.

ENVIRONMENT: Bathroom with mirror
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

03. raw-026 / Bending Nail Hammer
candidateId: sem1-23a3c991b8104bee

PROTAGONIST:
Kiko

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Hang a picture frame

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Hammer

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Hammering a nail drives it straight into wall.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Every hammer strike causes the nail to bend away from the wall at a right angle; bending increases with force.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Kiko hits nail; it curves 90 degrees without entering wall.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Hammer is meant to apply axial force to nail; if nail deflects under any force, it cannot penetrate.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action to hang frame fails as nail never goes straight, preventing secure mounting.

FRAME ZERO:
Kiko holds hammer mid-swing, but the nail protruding from the wall is bent into a U-shape, and the picture frame leans beside him.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Kiko holds the nail straight with pliers and hammers gently.
Visible consequence: The nail bends around the pliers and grips them, trapping the tool.
New information: The nail's bending is directional and can ensnare nearby tools.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Kiko uses a longer nail, hoping it will go straight.
Visible consequence: The long nail curls into a spiral, then springs back and pings off the wall.
New information: Nail length increases elasticity, causing projectile bending.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Kiko pre-drills a hole, then taps the nail.
Visible consequence: The nail passes halfway, then bends sideways and splits the wallboard, making a large hole.
New information: The nail bends at a specific depth, damaging the wall.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Kiko gives up on nails and uses adhesive strips.
Visible consequence: The hammer leaps from his hand, straightens the bent nail with one tap, then hammers it perfectly into the wall.
New information: The hammer can install nails only when not held by Kiko.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Kiko grabs the hammer to adjust the picture, but the hammer now bends every nail in the house.
Visible consequence: All nails in the room curve, pictures crash, and the original nail pings into Kiko's hand, bending his thumb comically.
New information: The hammer's bent-nail effect spreads to all previously straight nails.

FINAL PAYOFF:
All nails in the room curve, pictures crash, and the original nail pings into Kiko's hand, bending his thumb comically.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Any future hanging requires avoiding hammers, but the hammer may seek Kiko out.

ENVIRONMENT: Living room wall
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE

04. raw-027 / Opposite-Color Paintbrush
candidateId: sem1-2ae718e8672f18cc

PROTAGONIST:
Mimi

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Paint wall blue

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Paintbrush

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Brush applies paint color loaded on it.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Whatever color you load, the brush deposits the complementary color; loading blue paints orange, loading red paints green.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Mimi strokes blue paint; wall turns orange.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Brush is a transfer tool for pigment; if it inverts color, painting a chosen color is impossible.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action to apply blue paint produces orange, so the wall never becomes blue.

FRAME ZERO:
Mimi stands at a wall with a blue paint bucket and brush mid-stroke; the painted patch is vivid orange.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Mimi mixes a custom color equal parts blue and orange, hoping inversion cancels.
Visible consequence: The brush paints a shimmering gray that immediately splits into blue and orange stripes.
New information: Complementary inversion doesn't cancel but separates into two colors.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Mimi tries to paint the wall orange, expecting inversion to give blue.
Visible consequence: The wall turns blue-green, not pure blue, and the brush swirls automatically.
New information: The inversion rule includes a hue shift, so aiming for orange doesn't yield blue.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Mimi uses a roller instead of brush.
Visible consequence: The roller paints the opposite color at triple scale, covering the wall in a giant orange sunburst.
New information: The inversion applies to any painting tool, increasing area.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Mimi puts a stencil on the wall to paint a pattern.
Visible consequence: The stencil areas stay original color, while the exposed wall turns complementary, creating a negative of the pattern.
New information: The brush inverts color in the open areas, not just strokes.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Mimi gives up and paints the wall back white.
Visible consequence: The white paint turns black, then the entire wall flashes and becomes a mirror reflecting the room in inverted colors.
New information: Painting white triggers a full-wall color inversion, turning the wall into a funhouse mirror.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The white paint turns black, then the entire wall flashes and becomes a mirror reflecting the room in inverted colors.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Any new paint attempt may cause new color chaos; the wall can never be one color.

================================================================================================
05. raw-005 / Vacuum Blows
candidateId: sem1-913bc9ae39a3c15e

PROTAGONIST:
Luca

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Clean crumbs from carpet

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Vacuum cleaner

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Vacuum sucks air and debris into bag.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The vacuum blows air outward instead of sucking, scattering dust and crumbs toward the user's face and across the room.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Luca turns it on, crumbs fly up and coat his glasses.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Vacuum's core function is negative pressure; reversing polarity turns cleaning into a debris storm.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action meant to remove crumbs instead propels them everywhere, making the room dirtier and preventing cleaning.

FRAME ZERO:
Luca pushes a vacuum over a carpet, but a cloud of dust and crumbs is erupting from the nozzle, covering his shoes and floating upward.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Luca checks the bag and points nozzle away, turning it on again.
Visible consequence: A blast of air shoots out, knocking over a plant and spraying soil across the floor.
New information: The motor polarity is reversed, so it's now a leaf blower.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Luca reverses the vacuum's hose, attaching the other end to the front.
Visible consequence: The machine still blows, but now it sends air into its own bag, inflating it like a balloon until it pops.
New information: Reversing the hose doesn't change airflow; it just pressurizes the containment.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Luca holds the vacuum bag shut with his hand and tilts the nozzle toward a window.
Visible consequence: The bag inflates, lifts the vacuum off the floor, and starts flying around the room, spraying dust in circles.
New information: The trapped air turns the vacuum into a thruster, causing uncontrolled flight.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Luca unplugs the vacuum to stop the chaos.
Visible consequence: The vacuum falls to the floor and lets out a final puff of dust that settles exactly back onto the carpet.
New information: Without power, the debris returns to its original position due to the reversed air pressure.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Luca plugs it back in, but this time turns the power dial to 'max' hoping it will suck.
Visible consequence: The vacuum rockets forward, dragging Luca across the carpet and out the door, leaving a trail of sucked-up crumbs behind.
New information: Max power reverses thrust direction, so the vacuum now sucks only when moving backward, dragging the user.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The vacuum rockets forward, dragging Luca across the carpet and out the door, leaving a trail of sucked-up crumbs behind.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Each correction flips the airflow direction, but always in the opposite of what Luca expects, so he is alternately blown or dragged.

ENVIRONMENT: Living room
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

06. raw-006 / Mirrored Doorway
candidateId: sem1-d1e9d5ce1154b96b

PROTAGONIST:
Noah

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Enter the kitchen from hallway

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Door

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Opening a door leads to the room on the other side.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Whichever side of the door Noah opens, he steps into the same hallway but mirrored left-right.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Noah opens kitchen door and sees hallway behind him without turning around.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
A door is a threshold; if it teleports you back but mirrored, the transition is impossible to complete.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The act of crossing to the kitchen instead returns him to the hallway, preventing the goal.

FRAME ZERO:
Noah holds a door open; through the doorway is a reversed hallway identical to the one behind him, while a kitchen is visible in the mirror behind him.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Noah steps through the doorway quickly, expecting to enter kitchen.
Visible consequence: He lands in a mirrored hallway, with door now behind him showing the kitchen as a reflection.
New information: Passing through flips his orientation and swaps which side is real.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Noah backs through the doorway, hoping to reverse the mirror effect.
Visible consequence: He ends up in the original hallway but now the door is on his left instead of right, and the kitchen is behind him.
New information: Backing through does not cancel the flip; it just reorients his body.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Noah tries to open the door from the mirrored hallway's other side.
Visible consequence: The door opens to an identical mirrored hallway nested inside, and Noah sees multiple copies of himself standing in doorways.
New information: Each crossing creates a new mirrored copy of the same hallway, like an infinite recursion.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Noah holds the door open with one hand and reaches through to touch the kitchen counter he can see in the reflection.
Visible consequence: His arm disappears through the doorway and emerges from a door behind him, tapping his own shoulder.
New information: Space is folded so any object poked through wraps around to the starting side.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Noah closes the door entirely, then opens it again while looking at his feet.
Visible consequence: He steps through and finds himself in the kitchen, but everything is mirrored—cabinets open the wrong way, and the clock runs backward.
New information: The door finally allows entry, but the entire kitchen's handedness is reversed, so using it remains confusing.

FINAL PAYOFF:
He steps through and finds himself in the kitchen, but everything is mirrored—cabinets open the wrong way, and the clock runs backward.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Every additional door in the kitchen may also be mirrored, leading Noah to question every threshold.

07. raw-018 / Random Fridge Interior
candidateId: sem1-91ea45894fa28d7b

PROTAGONIST:
Noah

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Get milk from fridge

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Refrigerator

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Opening door reveals same contents each time.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Each time the fridge door opens, the interior is a different environment (a desert, a freezer landscape, a tropical beach, etc.), making food retrieval impossible.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Noah opens door expecting milk, sees a small snowstorm inside.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Fridge is a stable storage box; if interior is random, it can't store food.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action of opening to get milk reveals random non-milk content, preventing goal.

FRAME ZERO:
Noah holds fridge door open, but inside is a miniature glacier with a penguin; no shelves or milk carton.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Noah closes and reopens the fridge, hoping for the normal interior.
Visible consequence: Now the interior is a tropical rainforest with waterfalls; a pineapple rolls out.
New information: Each opening cycles through random biome, never the kitchen fridge.

Visible consequence: The environment morphs every three seconds, cycling through desert, ocean, wardrobe, and a copy of the kitchen seen from behind.
New information: The fridge's interior is a portal to other places, not a storage space.

BEAT 4

Visible consequence: The door closes and reopens, showing Noah inside a snowy tundra waving from behind a glass door in another country.
New information: Entering the fridge transports the person to the biome, not to the groceries.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Noah unplugs the fridge, expecting it to reset.
Visible consequence: The door pops open, and a blast of cold air covers the kitchen in frost; the milk carton appears on top of the fridge, frozen solid.
New information: Turning off power deposits the missing milk outside the fridge, but frozen and unusable.

The door pops open, and a blast of cold air covers the kitchen in frost; the milk carton appears on top of the fridge, frozen solid.

================================================================================================
08. raw-008 / Neighbor's TV Remote
candidateId: sem1-8a6fb30b4a11976b

PROTAGONIST:
Kiko

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Change channel to watch favorite show

PRIMARY OBJECT:
TV remote

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Pressing buttons changes your own TV channel.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The remote's signal always targets the neighbor's television through the wall, changing their channel while your TV stays off.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Kiko presses power; neighbor yells, but Kiko's TV remains black.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Remote is designed to control one specific TV; misdirecting signal to wrong TV makes it useless for your goal.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The intended action of controlling your TV instead controls someone else's, so your show cannot be selected.

FRAME ZERO:
Kiko points remote at his dark TV, but through the apartment wall, the neighbor's TV screen is visibly flickering channels, and the neighbor's shouting bubble appears.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Kiko moves closer to his TV, pressing power repeatedly.
Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV volume increases to maximum, shaking the wall, while Kiko's screen stays off.
New information: Distance doesn't matter; the remote's signal is hardwired to the neighbor's set.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Kiko covers the remote's IR emitter with his hand, hoping to block scatter.
Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV turns off and on rapidly, causing a strobe effect through the window.
New information: Blocking the emitter still sends an encoded signal that only the neighbor's TV understands.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Kiko opens his TV's manual buttons and tries to change channel directly.
Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV switches to a karaoke channel, and the neighbor starts singing loudly, while Kiko's TV ignores the manual buttons.
New information: Any input intended for Kiko's TV is transferred to the neighbor's TV due to a crossed signal wire.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Kiko unplugs his TV to 'reset' the remote link.
Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV turns off and then on, now showing Kiko's living room via a hidden camera feed.
New information: The link is now bidirectional; the neighbor's TV displays Kiko's actions.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Kiko goes next door to ask the neighbor to change their channel to Kiko's show.
Visible consequence: The neighbor agrees, but when they press their own remote, Kiko's TV turns on and changes to a cooking show.
New information: The cross-wiring is symmetrical: neighbor's remote controls Kiko's TV.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The neighbor agrees, but when they press their own remote, Kiko's TV turns on and changes to a cooking show.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Kiko could swap remotes with neighbor, but that might swap their TVs entirely, leading to endless confusion.

ENVIRONMENT: Apartment living room with thin wall to neighbor
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

09. raw-004 / Sweep-Back Broom
candidateId: sem1-11beb05a18d36fdb

PROTAGONIST:
Arda

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Sweep dirt into a dustpan

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Broom

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Sweeping moves dirt forward into a pile.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Dirt swept forward reappears in its original location exactly two seconds later, while the broom's bristles leave a clean trail that slowly fills back.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Arda sweeps a pile to the dustpan, turns to get pan, and the dirt is already back where it started.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Broom bristles are meant to displace particles; if the displacement is temporary, sweeping becomes a time-delayed loop.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action of sweeping creates only a brief illusion of cleanliness; dirt returns before it can be collected, preventing the goal.

FRAME ZERO:
Arda stands with broom in mid-sweep, a neat pile near dustpan; behind him, the original dirt patch is slowly reappearing on the floor.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Arda sweeps quickly and immediately tilts dustpan to catch pile.
Visible consequence: As he tilts pan, the pile dissolves and dirt races back to the original spot, leaving pan empty.
New information: The delay is exactly two seconds, and any pause lets dirt escape.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Arda sweeps while holding dustpan in the other hand, moving both together.
Visible consequence: Dirt enters the pan but then flies out backward through the bristles, returning to start.
New information: The broom's bristles repel dirt when the pan is close, so dirt cannot be trapped.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Arda turns the broom over and uses the handle to push dirt like a rake.
Visible consequence: The dirt stays put for a full three seconds, then jumps back even faster, scattering across a wider area.
New information: Using a non-bristle part changes the delay to three seconds but increases speed of return.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Arda sweeps dirt into an open shoebox and quickly closes the lid.
Visible consequence: The box shakes, dirt bursts out the seams, and returns to the original spot, leaving box empty.
New information: Containment fails; dirt always phases back to start regardless of barrier.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Arda gives up and simply walks away, leaving the broom on the floor.
Visible consequence: The broom starts sweeping itself in small arcs, and the dirt forms one neat pile in the center of the room.
New information: The broom can only sweep effectively when no one is trying to collect the dirt.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The broom starts sweeping itself in small arcs, and the dirt forms one neat pile in the center of the room.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Arda may return to collect, but then dirt scatters; the broom only succeeds when unattended.

ENVIRONMENT: Kitchen floor
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

10. raw-019 / Microwave Hot Plate
candidateId: sem1-63760a23881b08d8

PROTAGONIST:
Opa

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Reheat leftovers

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Microwave oven

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Microwave heats food evenly.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The microwave heats the plate to scalding while food remains frozen solid; any attempt to remove plate transfers heat to food, flash-thawing it but also melting plate.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Opa opens microwave, plate glows red, food has ice crystals.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Microwave's energy is meant to excite water in food; if it excites the plate material, food stays cold.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action of heating food results in dangerous hot plate and cold food, preventing eating.

FRAME ZERO:
Opa opens microwave, steam rises from the plate, but the food on it is visibly frozen with frost.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Opa uses oven mitts to remove the hot plate.
Visible consequence: The mitts start smoking, and the plate's heat melts a hole through the mitt, dropping the plate onto the counter, which sizzles.
New information: The plate is hotter than expected; it transfers heat outward.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Opa runs the plate under cold water to cool it.
Visible consequence: The water flashes to steam, and the food thaws instantly but is now soggy and boiling.
New information: Water contact transfers heat to food but also overcooks it.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Opa puts the frozen food on a different plate and microwaves again.
Visible consequence: The new plate heats red, food remains frozen, and the microwave's glass window fogs.
New information: The microwave always targets the plate material, not food.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Opa tries to microwave the food without a plate, directly on the turntable.
Visible consequence: The microwave refuses to start, beeping and displaying 'PLATE REQUIRED' in glowing letters.

11. raw-020 / Wrong-Room Light Switch
candidateId: sem1-c2b6225333ed3cda

PROTAGONIST:
Kiko

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Turn on living room light

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Light switch

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Flipping switch controls nearest room's light.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The switch controls the light in a different room (e.g., bathroom), while the room you're in stays dark; each switch in house controls a random other room.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Kiko flips living room switch; hall light turns on, living room dark.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Light switches are spatially tied to their room; cross-wiring makes them useless for local control.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action to illuminate your room instead lights a distant room, leaving you in dark.

FRAME ZERO:
Kiko stands in dark living room flipping a switch; through the doorway, the bathroom light is on, and the living room remains dark.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Kiko flips the switch repeatedly, hoping to get the right room.
Visible consequence: Various lights in the house turn on and off in a pattern, but the living room stays dark while the porch light blinks.
New information: The switch cycles through all other rooms except the one he's in.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Kiko walks to the bathroom to flip that switch.
Visible consequence: The bathroom light turns off, and the living room light turns on.
New information: Switches are paired with opposite rooms; you must be in the room you don't want lit.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Kiko stands in the doorway between rooms to flip both switches.
Visible consequence: Both rooms go dark, and a flickering light appears in the attic.
New information: Simultaneous toggles transfer power to an unswitched room.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Kiko uses a flashlight to see while navigating the switch panel.
Visible consequence: The flashlight turns on, but its beam illuminates the wrong room through a window, and Kiko stumbles.
New information: The cross-wiring extends to all light sources, including portable ones.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Kiko gives up and uses candles.
Visible consequence: The candles' flames cast shadows that point to a hidden switch behind a bookshelf; flipping that switch turns on the sun outside, causing full daylight indoors.
New information: There is a master switch that controls daylight, not indoor lights, solving the darkness but at absurd scale.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The candles' flames cast shadows that point to a hidden switch behind a bookshelf; flipping that switch turns on the sun outside, causing full daylight indoors.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
The daylight switch may reset at night, forcing Kiko to find another hidden control.

ENVIRONMENT: House interior, multiple rooms
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

12. raw-032 / Delayed Mirror
candidateId: sem1-cfeed66616185cb0

PROTAGONIST:
Kiko

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Fix hair using mirror

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Wall mirror

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Mirror shows real-time reflection.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The mirror shows a reflection delayed by one second, so Kiko sees her previous movement; when she moves to adjust, the lag causes her to misreach and bump herself.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Kiko raises hand, mirror's hand raises a second later.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Mirror's function is immediate visual feedback; delay makes self-grooming impossible.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action to style hair is based on outdated visual, so hands miss, preventing fixing.

FRAME ZERO:
Kiko stands before mirror, her hand raised, but the reflection's hand is still lowered; a ghostly lag overlay is visible.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Kiko moves slowly, watching the mirror to adjust to delay.
Visible consequence: The mirror's delay increases to three seconds, and her reflection starts combing hair that Kiko hasn't touched yet.
New information: The lag can grow when she tries to compensate.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Kiko touches her hair while looking down, ignoring the mirror.
Visible consequence: The mirror shows her reflection looking up and smiling, then her own head tilts involuntarily to match.
New information: The delayed reflection can control her current actions, not just display.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Kiko covers the mirror with a towel.
Visible consequence: The covered mirror continues to reflect, showing a delayed image on the towel's surface like a screen.
New information: The reflection can project onto coverings.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Kiko turns the mirror around to face the wall.
Visible consequence: The back of the mirror shows a live feed of Kiko from a second ago on the wall, and she sees herself walking backward.
New information: The mirror's delay effect transfers to the wall surface.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Kiko stands perfectly still until the reflection catches up.
Visible consequence: The reflection catches up and then freezes, while Kiko's own image splits into two identical Kikos in the room.
New information: Synchronizing the delay creates a duplicate Kiko, solving the grooming by having the duplicate fix her hair.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The reflection catches up and then freezes, while Kiko's own image splits into two identical Kikos in the room.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
The duplicate Kiko may also have a delayed reflection, causing infinite twins.

13. raw-003 / Spiraling Shopping Cart
candidateId: sem1-5cf30c7094c804b1

PROTAGONIST:
Mimi

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Push cart through supermarket to checkout

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Shopping cart

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
All four wheels swivel and roll straight when pushed.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
Each wheel locks in alternating directions every time the cart moves, causing cart to travel in tightening spirals toward the store entrance.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Mimi pushes forward, cart veers left and circles back.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Cart wheels are meant to provide straight mobility; locking their directions converts steering into a chaotic orbit.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The push intended to move cart forward instead causes a spiral away from checkout, preventing reaching the goal.

FRAME ZERO:
Mimi stands behind a shopping cart in an aisle, hands on handle, but all four wheels are pointed in different fixed angles; the cart has already begun a curved path back toward the entrance doors.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Mimi pushes harder and straightens handle to go down aisle.
Visible consequence: The cart jerks right, then left, tracing a spiral that wraps around a cereal display.
New information: Force increases turning radius instability, not direction control.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Mimi tries to pull the cart backward, hoping the wheels unlock.
Visible consequence: The cart spins in place like a top, cans rattling, then accelerates forward in a new spiral toward the produce section.
New information: Backward motion resets wheel angles to new random locks, worsening path.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Mimi leans over and tries to rotate one wheel by hand while holding cart steady.
Visible consequence: The other three wheels snap to new positions, causing the cart to lurch sideways and knock over a stack of oranges.
New information: Wheel directions are interdependent; adjusting one changes all.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Mimi abandons pushing and drags the cart along the floor by its front corner.
Visible consequence: The cart tilts and rolls on two wheels, then slams down and spirals toward the automatic entrance, triggering doors to open.
New information: The cart is stable only when moving sideways, but that still leads to exit.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Mimi lets go of the cart and runs toward checkout, hoping to grab items and leave.
Visible consequence: The cart, still spiraling, bumps into Mimi from behind, pushing her through the checkout line and out the exit.
New information: The cart's uncontrollable motion actively pursues Mimi, forcing her out of the store without paying.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The cart, still spiraling, bumps into Mimi from behind, pushing her through the checkout line and out the exit.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Any attempt to steer or adjust wheels only creates a new spiral, and the cart inevitably herds Mimi toward the exit.

ENVIRONMENT: Supermarket aisle with checkout and automatic doors
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

================================================================================================
14. raw-015 / Bottom-Opening Zipper
candidateId: sem1-90670eeaf4ee0468

PROTAGONIST:
Mimi

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Zip up jacket to stay warm

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Jacket zipper

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Pulling zipper up closes the jacket.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
As you pull the zipper tab upward, the bottom of the zipper separates, so the jacket opens from bottom while top closes; when you pull down, the bottom closes but top opens.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Mimi zips up to neck; the jacket's bottom half splits open, exposing her stomach.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Zipper is designed to interlock teeth sequentially; if it opens opposite end, the closure is incomplete.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action to close the jacket creates an opening elsewhere, so warmth cannot be retained.

FRAME ZERO:
Mimi holds zipper tab at neck, jacket top is closed, but the bottom half hangs open, cold air whooshing through.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Mimi pulls zipper down to close the bottom gap.
Visible consequence: The bottom zips shut but the top splits down to her chest.
New information: The zipper has a pivot point; one end opens as the other closes.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Mimi holds the bottom together with one hand while zipping up with the other.
Visible consequence: The zipper teeth at her hand pop apart, and her fingers get caught between them.
New information: Physical holding doesn't prevent separation; the zipper actively parts around any obstruction.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Mimi zips halfway, hoping the middle stays closed.
Visible consequence: Both top and bottom open outward like a mouth, and the jacket flaps open completely.
New information: Halfway position opens both ends simultaneously.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Mimi turns the jacket inside out and zips from the other side.
Visible consequence: The zipper now unzips from the middle, splitting the jacket into two separate halves that fall to the floor.
New information: The zipper's failure mode changes with orientation, now bisects the garment.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Mimi grabs a safety pin to pin the bottom closed.
Visible consequence: The safety pin zips itself into the zipper track and the entire jacket rolls up like a window blind, squeezing Mimi's torso.
New information: The zipper treats any inserted object as a pull tab and activates full closure upward, trapping Mimi.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The safety pin zips itself into the zipper track and the entire jacket rolls up like a window blind, squeezing Mimi's torso.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Further attempts to unfasten may cause the jacket to roll or unroll unpredictably.

ENVIRONMENT: Cold windy street
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

CURRENT VALIDATION RESULTS

Canonical ranking score: None

================================================================================================
15. raw-016 / Bottomless Backpack
candidateId: sem1-6c8721485468a0f0

PROTAGONIST:
Arda

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Pack backpack for school

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Backpack

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Items placed in top stay inside until you open it.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The bottom of the backpack opens whenever it is upright; when turned upside down, the bottom seals but the top opens, so contents always spill.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Arda puts books in upright backpack; they fall through bottom onto floor.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Backpack is a container; if gravity works against the orientation, it cannot hold items.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The act of placing items inside directly causes them to exit through bottom, preventing packing.

FRAME ZERO:
Arda holds a backpack upright with several books halfway falling through the open bottom, while the top zipper is closed.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Arda turns the backpack upside down to use the sealed bottom as top.
Visible consequence: The original top zipper opens, and books pour out onto his head.
New information: The pack has two openings, and gravity always finds the open one.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Arda holds the backpack sideways and loads books through the side.
Visible consequence: A new side seam splits open, and all contents slide out horizontally.
New information: Any orientation creates a new opening at the lowest point.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Arda stuffs clothes into the bottom to block it, then adds books.
Visible consequence: The clothes squeeze through the bottom seam, pulling the books with them, leaving an empty flattened pack.
New information: The bottom opening acts like a drain that vacuums contents out.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Arda puts the backpack on his back, hoping body pressure seals the bottom.
Visible consequence: The pack inflates and launches off his shoulders, spinning in midair as contents rainbow out.
New information: Wearing the pack triggers ejection, not sealing.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Arda gives up and carries books in his arms.
Visible consequence: The backpack slides over and swallows the books from his arms, then spits them out the bottom into a neat pile at his feet.
New information: The pack wants to be filled but immediately empties itself as soon as it contains anything.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The backpack slides over and swallows the books from his arms, then spits them out the bottom into a neat pile at his feet.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Any future loading attempt triggers the same drain; Arda might need a bottomless bag that is actually bottomless.

ENVIRONMENT: Bedroom floor
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

================================================================================================
16. raw-001 / The Inward Umbrella
candidateId: sem1-70ee2b7099c48d36

PROTAGONIST:
Opa

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Stay dry in the rain

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Umbrella

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Pressing the button opens the canopy above, creating a dry shield.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The umbrella opens downward, wrapping around the user's body instead of above it, and always migrates to cover the lowest point.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Opa presses the open button under rain; the canopy blooms down over his arm and torso, leaving his head wet.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
An umbrella's entire purpose is to open into a dome over your head; inverting that dome makes it wrap you in wet fabric while rain still hits your exposed head.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action meant to create dry overhead space instead traps the body and concentrates rain onto the uncovered head, directly preventing dryness.

FRAME ZERO:
Opa stands in rain with a closed umbrella raised; the button has been pressed but the canopy has opened downward around his arm and upper body, his head exposed to downpour.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Opa pulls the inverted canopy off his head and points the handle skyward, pressing button again to close.
Visible consequence: The wet fabric slides up his arm and seals around his neck like a collar, while rain soaks his exposed face.
New information: The umbrella can't be removed by pulling; it migrates to the highest point of his body.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Opa crouches low to the ground, hoping the umbrella will slide up to his raised hand.
Visible consequence: The canopy promptly engulfs his crouched back and legs, leaving only his feet sticking out, while rain pools on the fabric.
New information: The umbrella always moves to cover the lowest body part, not the highest.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Opa lies flat on the ground, spreading arms and legs to prevent any body part from being lowest.
Visible consequence: The umbrella flattens over his entire body like a tarp, but water runs off the edges and forms a puddle around him, soaking him from below.
New information: Being covered doesn't stop water; it collects and soaks from ground contact.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Opa rolls sideways toward a bus shelter, hoping to escape the rain while still covered.
Visible consequence: The umbrella rolls with him, wrapping around his torso like a soggy cocoon, and his head remains in the rain as he bumps into the shelter wall.
New information: The umbrella's coverage deflects all rain away from any part it touches, concentrating rain onto uncovered head.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Opa sits up inside the shelter, folds the umbrella manually by hand, and uses it as a normal hat.
Visible consequence: The umbrella stays open upside-down over his head, collecting rainwater that pours down his face inside the shelter.
New information: The impossible orientation persists even when manually adjusted; any use creates a personal rain cloud.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The umbrella stays open upside-down over his head, collecting rainwater that pours down his face inside the shelter.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Each new body position causes the umbrella to relocate to the lowest point, so Opa keeps trying to out-think the wrap but only changes which part gets covered.

ENVIRONMENT: Rainy city sidewalk
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

================================================================================================
17. raw-009 / Ceiling Pancake
candidateId: sem1-dd72ae9a5bebad5a

PROTAGONIST:
Mimi

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Flip a pancake

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Frying pan

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
A quick wrist flip sends pancake up and back into pan.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The pan exerts a vertical launch force so strong that any food flies upward and sticks to the ceiling; the pan then becomes too slippery to hold.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Mimi flips the pancake; it rockets to ceiling and stays.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Pan's function is to toss food for cooking; if toss becomes permanent adhesion, cooking is impossible.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The flipping action meant to turn the pancake instead removes it from the pan and glues it overhead, preventing eating.

FRAME ZERO:
Mimi holds a frying pan, looking up; a pancake is stuck flat on the ceiling, and a second pancake arcs upward from the pan.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Mimi scrapes the pancake off the ceiling with a spatula, then tries a gentler flip.
Visible consequence: The pancake still leaps up and splats on ceiling, sticking even harder.
New information: The launch force is independent of wrist strength.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Mimi holds a plate over the pan and flips, hoping to catch the pancake.
Visible consequence: The pancake punches through the plate, sending shards flying, and both pancake and plate fragments stick to ceiling.
New information: The launched food has enough force to penetrate obstacles.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Mimi turns off the stove and waits for the pancake to fall.
Visible consequence: The pancake slowly peels off the ceiling and falls back into the pan, but immediately turns black and smokes.
New information: The pancake's fall is controlled by cooking timer, not gravity; it returns overcooked.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Mimi tries to cook without flipping, using a lid to steam.
Visible consequence: The pan's handle starts vibrating, and all the batter in the pan spontaneously flips itself to the ceiling in one big sheet.
New information: The pan's flipping rule applies to any batter inside, even without a wrist motion.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Mimi gives up and turns off the stove, leaving the pan on the counter.
Visible consequence: The pan itself flips upward, sticks to the ceiling, and the pancake falls onto Mimi's head.
New information: The pan's upward launch now applies to itself, completing the role reversal.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The pan itself flips upward, sticks to the ceiling, and the pancake falls onto Mimi's head.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
Any cooking utensil in this kitchen may also launch upward, forcing Mimi to cook with ceiling-mounted tools.

ENVIRONMENT: Kitchen with stove
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

================================================================================================
18. raw-031 / Shadow Flashlight
candidateId: sem1-c85a677474d9ed27

PROTAGONIST:
Opa

EVERYDAY GOAL:
Find keys in dark room

PRIMARY OBJECT:
Flashlight

NORMAL OBJECT EXPECTATION:
Flashlight produces light beam to illuminate.

IMPOSSIBLE RULE:
The flashlight absorbs light and projects a darkness beam; pointing it at objects makes them disappear into shadow, while surroundings stay dim.

VISIBLE TRIGGER:
Opa turns on flashlight, a black cone shoots out, making the area darker.

WHY THIS EXACT OBJECT:
Flashlight's purpose is to emit light; emitting darkness is its inverse.

GOAL–RULE RELATIONSHIP:
The action to illuminate keys instead casts them into deeper shadow, preventing search.

FRAME ZERO:
Opa points a flashlight at the floor, but a black beam covers the area where keys should be, while the rest of the room is faint.

BEAT 1
Action / strategy: Opa shakes the flashlight and changes batteries.
Visible consequence: The darkness beam becomes wider, swallowing a chair and half the room into blackness.
New information: More power increases darkness, not light.

BEAT 2
Action / strategy: Opa points the flashlight at the ceiling, hoping reflected light reveals keys.
Visible consequence: The ceiling turns black and starts dripping shadow like ink, covering the floor.
New information: The darkness is a substance that can drip and spread.

BEAT 3
Action / strategy: Opa turns on a lamp instead.
Visible consequence: The lamp works normally, but the flashlight's shadow beam seeks out the lamp and extinguishes it.
New information: The darkness beam actively cancels any other light source.

BEAT 4
Action / strategy: Opa uses the flashlight in reverse, pointing it at his own eyes.
Visible consequence: Instead of darkness, a bright beam shines out the back of the flashlight, illuminating a corner where keys are visible.
New information: The flashlight has reversed polarity: front emits dark, back emits light.

BEAT 5
Action / strategy: Opa picks up the keys using the light from the back.
Visible consequence: The keys glow and float into the flashlight's front beam, where they vanish into shadow, never to be found.
New information: The flashlight's front beam can erase objects, not just darken them.

FINAL PAYOFF:
The keys glow and float into the flashlight's front beam, where they vanish into shadow, never to be found.

LOOP / REWATCH POTENTIAL (provider declaration):
The keys might reappear if Opa uses the back beam, but then the front beam may erase them again.

ENVIRONMENT: Dark hallway
DIALOGUE NEED: NONE
DURATION: 15

CURRENT VALIDATION RESULTS

Canonical ranking score: None

raw-007 / Backward Alarm Clock
ONE-SENTENCE PREMISE:
The clock's hands move backward whenever the alarm is set, so time appears to run in reverse; the alarm rings only when the hands return to midnight.
FRAME-ZERO HOOK:
Opa sits up in bed, staring at a bedside clock whose hour hand is moving backward past 6, while the alarm hand points to 7.
PROGRESSION:
1. As soon as he releases, the hands spin backward rapidly to 3:00.
2. The hands freeze, then twitch backward one minute for every real second that passes.
3. The alarm rings immediately, but the hands jump forward to noon, and the ringing reverses itself into a silent vacuum of sound.
4. The clock's display reappears on his phone screen, showing the same backward time, and the phone's alarm icon turns into an hourglass.
5. The clock chimes twelve times in reverse, and Opa wakes up at 7 AM, but the clock reads 7 PM.
PAYOFF:
The clock chimes twelve times in reverse, and Opa wakes up at 7 AM, but the clock reads 7 PM.
